=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a simple directed graph G with N vertices and N+M edges. The vertices are numbered 1 to N, and the edges are numbered 1 to N+M.
Edge i (1 \leq i \leq N) goes from vertex i to vertex i+1. (Here, vertex N+1 is considered as vertex 1.)
Edge N+i (1 \leq i \leq M) goes from vertex X_i to vertex Y_i.
Takahashi is at vertex 1. At each vertex, he can move to any vertex to which there is an outgoing edge from the current vertex.
Compute the number of ways he can move exactly K times.
That is, find the number of integer sequences (v_0, v_1, \dots, v_K) of length K+1 satisfying all of the following three conditions:

- 1 \leq v_i \leq N for i = 0, 1, \dots, K.
- v_0 = 1.
- There is a directed edge from vertex v_{i-1} to vertex v_i for i = 1, 2, \ldots, K.

Since this number can be very large, print it modulo 998244353.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M K
X_1 Y_1
X_2 Y_2
\vdots
X_M Y_M

Output

Print the count modulo 998244353.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 0 \leq M \leq 50
- 1 \leq K \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq X_i, Y_i \leq N, X_i \neq Y_i
- All of the N+M directed edges are distinct.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

6 2 5
1 4
2 5

Sample Output 1

5


The above figure represents the graph G. There are five ways for Takahashi to move:

- Vertex 1 \to Vertex 2 \to Vertex 3 \to Vertex 4 \to Vertex 5 \to Vertex 6
- Vertex 1 \to Vertex 2 \to Vertex 5 \to Vertex 6 \to Vertex 1 \to Vertex 2
- Vertex 1 \to Vertex 2 \to Vertex 5 \to Vertex 6 \to Vertex 1 \to Vertex 4
- Vertex 1 \to Vertex 4 \to Vertex 5 \to Vertex 6 \to Vertex 1 \to Vertex 2
- Vertex 1 \to Vertex 4 \to Vertex 5 \to Vertex 6 \to Vertex 1 \to Vertex 4

Sample Input 2

10 0 200000

Sample Output 2

1

Sample Input 3

199 10 1326
122 39
142 49
164 119
197 127
188 145
69 80
6 120
24 160
18 154
185 27

Sample Output 3

451022766

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, need think about correctness of compressed transitions more formally. We should ensure all walks correspond to paths in compressed graph and partial counts. Let's prove to ourselves.

At any time when at a source vertex, the next step can be either:
- cycle edge to v+1. Then until the next source (exclusive), all steps are forced cycle edges. The first source reached is c = next_source_exclusive(u), after L=(c-u mod N) steps (if c=u, L=N). This is a compressed transition.
- one of the extra edges from u to y. After this one step, if y is source, we are at source y; else continue forced cycle until first source c = next_source_inclusive(y). Total L=1+(c-y mod N). This is a compressed transition.
Between sources, no choices. Thus any walk is uniquely a sequence of compressed transitions, starting from vertex 1. If vertex 1 is not source, before first source it is a forced initial segment. If K falls inside a compressed transition, the endpoint is determined by the transition and offset; for each path prefix and transition, at most one endpoint at each offset. So counting via DP and partial condition is exact.

Need consider if at a source, choosing cycle edge could immediately land at another source, L=1. The compressed transition length 1. Then at time T+1 at source. Good. If choosing extra edge to target y that is source, L=1. Then at time T+1 at source. Good. If both cycle and extra edge from same source to same next source with same length, they are distinct transitions and counted separately. Our transition list includes cycle once and each extra edge separately. Good.

Need implement `next_source_exclusive(v)` efficiently. Since S<=50, can just linear scan sorted list:
```
def next_source_exclusive(v):
    # first source > v, else sources[0]
    for s in sources:
        if s > v:
            return s
    return sources[0]
```
Because sources sorted ascending. If v is source and there is another source >v, returns next; if v is largest, returns smallest. If v is not source and there is source >v, returns first >v; else smallest. This matches "after v" in cyclic order. If S=1 and s=v, no s>v, returns sources[0]=v. Good. O(S) per call, S<=50, transitions <=100, negligible.

This partitions all walks by the last transition that was started but not completed? Let's formalize:
For any walk, consider the sequence of compressed transitions completed before time K. Let T be the time at the last completed transition's destination source (or initial source). If T=K, counted by dp[T] at T=K. If T<K, then the walk has started a compressed transition from that source and has taken rem=K-T steps along it. The endpoint is unique for given transition and rem. Thus one-to-one. Good.

Now, let's think about if there
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
from array import array

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N, M, K = map(int, input().split())
    edges = []
    sources_set = set()
    for _ in range(M):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((x, y))
        sources_set.add(x)

    if M == 0:
        print(1)
        return

    sources = sorted(sources_set)
    S = len(sources)
    idx = {v: i for i, v in enumerate(sources)}

    def next_source_exclusive(v):
        # first source strictly after v in cyclic order 1,2,...,N
        for s in sources:
            if s > v:
                return s
        return sources[0]

    def next_source_inclusive(v):
        if v in sources_set:
            return v
        return next_source_exclusive(v)

    # Build compressed transitions.
    # State: being at a source vertex (a vertex with at least one extra outgoing edge).
    trans = [[] for _ in range(S)]

    for u in sources:
        ui = idx[u]

        # Take the normal cycle edge from u.
        c = next_source_exclusive(u)
        L = (c - u) % N
        if L == 0:
            L = N  # only source
        trans[ui].append((idx[c], L))

        # Take each extra edge from u.
        for x, y in edges:
            if x == u:
                c = next_source_inclusive(y)
                L = 1 + (c - y) % N
                trans[ui].append((idx[c], L))

    # dp[state][t] = number of ways to be at that source at time t
    dp = [array('I', [0]) * (K + 1) for _ in range(S)]

    # Initial segment from vertex 1 to the first source.
    if 1 in sources_set:
        dp[idx[1]][0] = 1
    else:
        s0 = next_source_exclusive(1)
        d0 = (s0 - 1) % N
        if K < d0:
            print(1)
            return
        dp[idx[s0]][d0] = 1

    ans = 0

    for t in range(K + 1):
        rem = K - t
        for si in range(S):
            val = dp[si][t]
            if val == 0:
                continue

            if t == K:
                ans += val
                if ans >= MOD:
                    ans -= MOD
                continue

            for dest, L in trans[si]:
                if L > rem:
                    # Stop inside this transition.
                    ans += val
                    if ans >= MOD:
                        ans -= MOD
                else:
                    # Complete this transition and arrive at a source.
                    nt = t + L
                    nv = dp[dest][nt] + val
                    if nv >= MOD:
                        nv -= MOD
                    dp[dest][nt] = nv

    print(ans % MOD)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
